## Detailed Assessments

### GMR Power and Urban Infra Ltd (GMRP&UI) - 3.7 / 10 - SERIOUS
*Sector: Power Generation / Smart Metering & Urban Infra - Confidence: Moderate - Updated: 2026-09-04*

**Summary:** Two critical flags — a 60%+ promoter pledge backed by promoter-level debt with 0.19x cover, and thin debt service coverage (ISCR 1.22x) on ₹9,300 Cr net debt at a BBB rating — plus low-quality, settlement-driven earnings. Operational improvement (PLFs, deleveraging, Infomerics upgrade) is real but does not offset the ownership and leverage risks.

| Category (weight) | Score | Key reason |
|---|:---:|---|
| Accounting (30%) | 4 | FY26 PAT ₹614 Cr versus other income ₹1,528 Cr (FY25: PAT ₹1,552 Cr vs other income ₹2,228 Cr) — reported profits are driven by settlements and monetisation, not operations; erratic effective tax rates (5,147% in Q4 FY26); smart-meter revenue recognised under Ind AS (₹224.5 Cr in Q1 FY27) diverges sharply from the company's own proforma (₹87.4 Cr); overdue receivables ₹680 Cr (₹500 Cr DFCC) and ₹2,829 Cr of claims under litigation. |
| Governance (30%) | 3 | 60% of promoter shares pledged (Jun-26 SHP) with a fresh 11 Jun 2026 pledge of 3.27% of capital for ₹1,400 Cr promoter debt at 0.19x cover; promoter stake diluted 13.6 pts in two years; related-party debt (57% of FY27 repayments to promoter/group companies); contingent liabilities ₹2,917 Cr; zero dividend history. Disclosure quality and the Bosch partnership are mitigants. |
| Financial (20%) | 3 | Net debt ₹9,300 Cr at 30 Jun 2026 (gross ₹11,400 Cr, cash ₹2,100 Cr) against Mar-26 equity of ~₹1,900 Cr; consolidated ISCR 1.22x in FY26 (1.06x FY25); gearing 9.09x; rating only IVR BBB/Stable; FY27 debt maturities ₹1,489 Cr vs FY26 CFO ₹1,260 Cr with reliance on asset sales; net loss in three of the last four quarters. |
| Business (20%) | 5 | Coal-fired IPPs with PPAs and high PLFs provide contracted cash flow, but exposure to discom receivables and coal prices; smart metering is a tender-based, capital-intensive business with extended timelines; EPC order book depleted after DFCC handover; highway claims and land monetisation are litigation- and counterparty-dependent. |

**Critical flags:**
- Promoter pledge 60% of holding (Jun-26 SHP), increased by the 11 Jun 2026 pledge of 3.27% of capital for ₹1,400 Cr promoter-level debt at 0.19x security cover
- Consolidated ISCR 1.22x (FY26), net debt ₹9,300 Cr at 30 Jun 2026, rating IVR BBB/Stable; FY26 PAT relied on ₹1,528 Cr other income

**Amber flags:**

- Promoter holding 34.63%, diluted from 39.31% (Mar-2018); FIIs 1.76%

*Sector: Chemicals / Electrical Insulation Materials - Confidence: Moderate - Updated: 2026-09-04*

**Summary:** No critical flags: debt-free, cash-rich and cash-generative. Amber items concern minority treatment (tiny payout, idle cash, no investor communication), earnings reliance on other income, the unexplained Q2 CY26 margin spike and a short NSE trading history.

| Category (weight) | Score | Key reason |
|---|:---:|---|
| Accounting (30%) | 7 | CY25 CFO Rs 127 Cr vs PAT Rs 148 Cr (86%); CFO/operating profit 97% in CY25 (71% in CY24 on working-capital build); debtor days steady at 60, cash conversion cycle 70 days. Earnings include material other income (Rs 48 Cr of Rs 199 Cr CY25 PBT; Rs 19.27 Cr in Q2 CY26). No exceptional items or auditor qualifications found. |
| Governance (30%) | 6 | 75% MNC parent with no pledge and no regulatory issues, but minority interests are secondary: 3-4% dividend payout, no earnings calls, unverified parent royalty flows, and public float of only 12.67%. Neutral-minus. |
| Financial (20%) | 8 | Zero borrowings in every reported year; investments Rs 642 Cr and reserves Rs 1,096 Cr at Jun-2026; interest cost Rs 0.12 Cr per quarter; FCF positive in 11 of the last 12 years (CY24 negative only due to the plant build). |
| Business (20%) | 6 | Demand concentrated in magnet-wire/transformer/motor customers tied to power and appliance cycles; raw materials are petrochemical derivatives with volatile pricing; the 29% OPM in Q2 CY26 versus 19-22% previously signals margin volatility in both directions. Import competition from Chinese resin producers is a structural risk. |

**Amber flags:**
- Dividend payout only 3-4% of PAT (CY22-CY25) despite zero debt and Rs 642 Cr investments at Jun-2026
- No earnings calls or management commentary; Q2 CY26 margin jump to 29% OPM unexplained
- Other income Rs 48 Cr = 24% of CY25 PBT (treasury income)
- NSE listing only since 20 Apr 2026 - short ELANTAS.NS price history; public float 12.67%, FII 0.05%
- Valuation 54.6x P/E and 8.8x book (4 Sep 2026)

*Sources: screener.in ELANTAS standalone tables and ratios (fetched 2026-09-04); moneycontrol.com Q2 CY26 P&L table (7 Aug 2026)*

*Sector: Auto Components / Batteries & Energy Storage - Confidence: Moderate - Updated: 2026-09-04*

**Summary:** No critical flags. The single material amber theme is the Rs 5,600 Cr lithium-ion project: delayed, over budget and without contracted offtake, it will depress consolidated returns until utilisation ramps. Core accounting, governance and liquidity are clean and AAA-rated.

| Category (weight) | Score | Key reason |
|---|:---:|---|
| Accounting (30%) | 7 | Cash conversion strong: FY26 CFO Rs 2,413 Cr vs PAT Rs 860 Cr (CFO/EBITDA 150%), cash conversion cycle 42 days, debtor days 32. Capitalised Li-ion costs (CWIP Rs 4,198 Cr) will start depreciating from commissioning, a visible but non-accounting drag. No qualification or restatement found. |
| Governance (30%) | 7 | Stable promoter, no pledge, AAA rating, independent-heavy board, clean disclosure record. No adverse finding. |
| Financial (20%) | 7 | Gross debt Rs 1,575 Cr (incl. Rs 482 Cr leases) versus Rs 1,082 Cr cash/liquid investments and a Rs 5,100 Cr HDFC Life stake at Mar-2026; FY27 capex Rs 1,400 Cr funded from operating cash flow with Rs 2,000 Cr of undrawn EESL term loans as backstop (ICRA). Leverage will rise modestly as EESL draws debt; ICRA expects metrics to stay strong. |
| Business (20%) | 6 | Named concerns: Li-ion offtake risk without take-or-pay contracts, import dependence for cell materials, technology obsolescence, competition from imports and new Indian gigafactories; lead-acid faces unorganised competition and cyclical industrial end-markets; lead recycling carries environmental compliance risk. **Amber flags:**
- Li-ion Phase 1 cost overrun (Rs 5,200 Cr to Rs 5,600 Cr) and commissioning delay to Sep-2026; no take-or-pay offtake (ICRA 11 Jun 2026)
- Consolidated ROCE 9% for FY25-FY26 with Rs 4,198 Cr CWIP yet to earn

- Raw-material import dependence and West Asia conflict impact flagged by ICRA
